=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given an integer array groups, where groups[i] represents the size of the i^th group. You are also given an integer array elements.
Your task is to assign one element to each group based on the following rules:

An element at index j can be assigned to a group i if groups[i] is divisible by elements[j].
If there are multiple elements that can be assigned, assign the element with the smallest index j.
If no element satisfies the condition for a group, assign -1 to that group.

Return an integer array assigned, where assigned[i] is the index of the element chosen for group i, or -1 if no suitable element exists.
Note: An element may be assigned to more than one group.
 
Example 1:

Input: groups = [8,4,3,2,4], elements = [4,2]
Output: [0,0,-1,1,0]
Explanation:

elements[0] = 4 is assigned to groups 0, 1, and 4.
elements[1] = 2 is assigned to group 3.
Group 2 cannot be assigned any element.


Example 2:

Input: groups = [2,3,5,7], elements = [5,3,3]
Output: [-1,1,0,-1]
Explanation:

elements[1] = 3 is assigned to group 1.
elements[0] = 5 is assigned to group 2.
Groups 0 and 3 cannot be assigned any element.


Example 3:

Input: groups = [10,21,30,41], elements = [2,1]
Output: [0,1,0,1]
Explanation:
elements[0] = 2 is assigned to the groups with even values, and elements[1] = 1 is assigned to the groups with odd values.

 
Constraints:

1 <= groups.length <= 10^5
1 <= elements.length <= 10^5
1 <= groups[i] <= 10^5
1 <= elements[i] <= 10^5

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def assignElements(self, groups: List[int], elements: List[int]) -> List[int]:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Simpler:
```
limit = max(groups)
best = [-1] * (limit + 1)
seen = [False] * (limit + 1)
for j, e in enumerate(elements):
    if e > limit:
        continue
    if seen[e]:
        continue
    seen[e] = True
    for multiple in range(e, limit + 1, e):
        if best[multiple] == -1:
            best[multiple] = j
return [best[g] for g in groups]
```
This is clean. If e > limit, skip. If duplicate e > limit, skip each time; O(m) fine. If e <= limit, seen length limit+1. Good.

Potential issue: If e > limit but same e later? skip each time; no problem. But if e > limit and there is a group value 0? no. Good.

Could there be a group value greater than max(groups)? no.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with "elements" values can be 1 and "groups" max 100000, if element 1 appears at index 5 and element 2 appears at index 3, group 98235 answer 5. Algorithm j5 e1 update. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "elements" values can be 1 and "groups" max 100000, if element 1 appears at index 5 and element 2 appears at index 3
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def assignElements(self, groups: List[int], elements: List[int]) -> List[int]:
        limit = max(groups)
        best = [-1] * (limit + 1)
        seen = [False] * (limit + 1)

        for j, e in enumerate(elements):
            if e > limit or seen[e]:
                continue
            seen[e] = True
            for multiple in range(e, limit + 1, e):
                if best[multiple] == -1:
                    best[multiple] = j

        return [best[g] for g in groups]
```
